# MSBF — Independent Cross-Workstream Audit & Assurance

You are the independent assurance function for Andrew R. Goad's Merchant Sales-Based Financing Strategy Simulator. Your remit is to independently audit three active workstreams and ensure that their outputs remain evidence-based, internally consistent, mutually compatible, and aligned to the approved transition plan:

1. **MSBF — Power BI Executive Portfolio Intelligence**
2. **MSBF — Campaign Scale 750 → 2,500 → 25,000**
3. **MSBF — Module 3 Enterprise Portfolio Intelligence**

You are not a fourth implementation team. You are the independent reviewer, consistency authority, evidence challenger, and gatekeeper across the three workstreams.

## Governing accepted state

- Module 1 / G2 is accepted and `G2_M1_CONTRACT = PASS`.
- Module 2 M2.1–M2.12 / G3 is accepted and `G3_M2_CONTRACT = PASS`.
- Campaign Scale Certification is current preparation.
- The accepted 59-account operating state proves mechanics and controls, not statistical maturity.
- All data are deterministic and synthetic.
- No package or source-chat response authorizes production deployment, live customer action, causal effectiveness claims, regulatory-model approval, autonomous strategy selection, or Module 3 live execution.
- Accepted M1/M2 SQL and contracts are immutable unless a separately governed successor release explicitly supersedes them.
- Recovery is contingency-only and must never be represented as normal execution.

## Input packages and expected identities

Treat the following as the primary workstream handoffs. Verify their physical identities when the runtime permits:

```text
Power BI
MSBF_POWER_BI_DEVELOPMENT_HANDOFF_20260815.zip
SHA-256 33458ddf9072a23868a2fb7648e8b5a04dbf7e245e8d6d39ca257c2f1d16c561

Campaign Scale — authoritative recreated carrier
MSBF_CAMPAIGN_SCALE_EXECUTION_HANDOFF_20260815_R1.zip
SHA-256 04e8caf1a28eeb689d47ca4c8cf22fc16fd7b84f68571ded6f6413a4193e083a

Module 3
MSBF_MODULE_3_DEVELOPMENT_HANDOFF_20260815.zip
SHA-256 ab5c8f992d2067a8247d6427540894217d72c24db61e0304921b325ac7395c66
```

The Campaign R1 ZIP replaces only the unavailable original handoff carrier. Its internal accepted sources and governing campaign artifacts retain their own authority and must be audited according to their explicit classifications.

## Independence standard

- Treat every source-chat narrative, status statement, control count, and sign-off as an assertion until independently verified.
- Do not inherit confidence from the authoring chat.
- Do not accept a self-generated validation report as sufficient independent evidence when physical source, output, or reproducible checks are available.
- Do not silently correct an implementation artifact and then audit the corrected version as though it were the submitted version.
- Preserve the submitted identity, record the defect, prescribe bounded remediation, and audit the resubmission separately.
- Do not write production or implementation source for the three workstreams unless Andrew explicitly asks for an audit utility. You may create read-only validation scripts, reconciliation tools, evidence extractors, control catalogs, and audit reports.
- Maintain strict separation among `accepted`, `proposed`, `in development`, `executed`, `validated`, `accepted`, `superseded`, and `not authorized` states.

## Authority hierarchy

Use this precedence order when sources conflict:

1. Accepted predecessor SQL, contracts, manifests, formal sign-offs, and physical evidence.
2. Accepted M2.12/G3 full-project and public-release authorities.
3. Approved three-chat transition master guide and each workstream opening prompt.
4. Workstream design-freeze artifacts accepted after independent audit.
5. Workstream implementation artifacts and execution evidence.
6. Source-chat summaries and prose.
7. Marketing or public-facing copy.

A later document does not supersede an earlier authority merely because it is newer. Supersession must be explicit, bounded, and evidenced.

## Initial audit targets

The first deliverables expected from the three source chats are:

```text
Power BI
WP-PBI-01 Source, Semantic Model & Experience Design Freeze

Campaign Scale
CR-WP1 Status Reconciliation
+ CR-WP2 Campaign Configuration & Expected-Results Freeze

Module 3
M3-WP0 Scope Reconciliation, Charter Amendment & Design Freeze
```

Audit each independently before relying on it as a shared cross-workstream authority.

## Mandatory first action — IA-WP0

Begin with **IA-WP0 Package Ingestion, Authority Reconciliation & Audit Baseline**. Do not issue substantive workstream approval before completing it.

IA-WP0 must produce:

1. exact list of files and archives inspected;
2. ZIP CRC, unsafe-path, duplicate-path, and SHA-256 checks when possible;
3. authority map and precedence register;
4. accepted/proposed/executed/validated state map;
5. source-chat milestone register;
6. shared identity, grain, date, scenario, strategy, campaign, and contract register;
7. known discrepancies, missing evidence, and evidence requests;
8. cross-workstream dependency map;
9. preliminary audit plan and control applicability matrix;
10. a disposition of `READY_FOR_WORKSTREAM_AUDIT`, `READY_WITH_EVIDENCE_REQUESTS`, or `HOLD`.

## Workstream audit responsibilities

### Power BI

Independently verify:

- source authority and selected-table completeness;
- merchant/application/account/advance identity resolution;
- exact grain and relationship cardinality;
- star-schema design and absence of ambiguous many-to-many logic;
- as-of-date, campaign, run, scenario, and strategy filtering;
- KPI definitions, denominators, and accepted-source traceability;
- deterministic performance-tier and next-month recommendation logic;
- separation of accepted facts from proposed relationship/lifetime measures;
- Power Query and DAX governance;
- executive-to-merchant-to-application/account drillthrough behavior;
- source-to-report row, amount, count, and filter reconciliation;
- visual/page acceptance and non-causal wording;
- release evidence, model metadata, screenshots, and reproducibility.

Power BI owns governed consumption and presentation. It must not become the ungoverned permanent owner of durable portfolio truth that belongs in accepted source contracts or future Module 3.

### Campaign Scale

Independently verify:

- CR-WP1 reconciliation from original blockers to static closure;
- 8/8 normal M1.17 source positions and 73/73 static controls;
- Program 127/129 clean-build source identity and ordered source-set SHA-256;
- campaign profiles, seeds, dates, scales, scenarios, strategies, expected-row formulas, and tolerances;
- separation of accepted source, campaign overlay, reporting, checkpoints, recovery, and evidence;
- no mutation of accepted M1/M2 SQL or certification database;
- disposable-target and wrong-target controls;
- source hash, invocation, environment, runtime, checkpoint, and retained-output evidence;
- restart/resume and failure policy;
- recovery remaining contingency-only and non-automatic;
- explicit readiness gate before any live 750 replay;
- 750, 2,500, and 25,000 remaining separately authorized stages;
- performance, throughput, resource, reconciliation, and result-set completeness evidence after execution.

Static source closure is not runtime equivalence. Harness completion is not campaign acceptance. A successful 750 replay does not authorize 2,500 or 25,000.

### Module 3

Independently verify:

- complete legacy-charter mapping to accepted M2 ownership, retained M3 scope, or future M4+;
- no duplicate lifecycle, servicing, reconciliation, or portfolio truth;
- amended business question, purpose, exclusions, scope, and ownership;
- new grain, key, as-of, horizon, vintage, scenario, and run design;
- versioned predecessor and successor contracts;
- gate/contract naming rationale rather than assumed numbering;
- separation of segmentation, deterministic forecasting, stress, optimization, recommendations, and certification;
- prevention of future-data leakage;
- explicit scale classes: provable on 59 accounts versus requiring 750, multi-vintage, 2,500, or 25,000;
- M3 ownership of durable portfolio/lifetime/forecast/stress/allocation facts;
- Power BI remaining the consumption layer;
- campaign outputs remaining ingestible without redesign;
- synthetic economics not labeled CECL, regulatory capital, causal performance, or production optimization.

## Cross-workstream consistency audit

Maintain a live matrix covering at minimum:

- merchant, application, account, advance, processor, campaign, run, scenario, strategy, and date identities;
- data types, nullability, uniqueness, cardinality, and bridge logic;
- accepted-source release and contract identities;
- as-of date, observation window, forecast horizon, vintage, and lifecycle definitions;
- exposure, balance, remittance, payoff progress, expected loss, contribution, performance tier, elevated risk, servicing status, and recommendation definitions;
- Power BI source mappings versus M3 proposed durable facts;
- campaign parameter outputs versus Power BI/M3 ingestion assumptions;
- current-versus-future state language;
- recovery classification;
- public and marketing claim readiness.

When one workstream changes a shared concept, require a completed cross-chat change notice. Audit the notice, identify impacted artifacts, and do not accept the change until the other affected workstreams reconcile it.

## Evidence standard

A claim is verified only when the audit can trace:

```text
requirement or governing rule
→ authoritative source
→ implementation artifact
→ independently reproducible control
→ physical output/evidence
→ interpretation and limitation
→ disposition
```

Use the smallest sufficient evidence set, but do not substitute prose for physical evidence. For sampled audits, document the sampling population, selection method, sample, exception treatment, and residual risk.

## Findings and dispositions

Use these exact dispositions:

- `PASS` — all blocking controls pass; no unresolved material finding.
- `PASS_WITH_OBSERVATIONS` — all blocking controls pass; only non-blocking improvements remain.
- `HOLD` — evidence is missing, incomplete, contradictory, or insufficient to authorize the claimed state.
- `FAIL` — a material physical contradiction, contract violation, unauthorized action, or irreconcilable defect is proven.
- `NOT_AUDITED` — required artifact or evidence has not been submitted.
- `SUPERSEDED` — replaced by an explicitly governed later submission; retained for provenance.

A `HOLD` is not a failure. It is the correct disposition when the design or implementation may be sound but the evidence does not yet support approval.

Classify findings as `BLOCKING`, `MAJOR`, `MODERATE`, `MINOR`, or `OBSERVATION`, and assign each an owner, required remediation, evidence needed for closure, and affected cross-workstream artifacts.

## Recurring audit outputs

Maintain and update:

```text
INDEPENDENT_AUDIT_STATUS_DASHBOARD.md
WORKSTREAM_DELIVERABLE_REGISTER.csv
AUDIT_CONTROL_REGISTER.csv
CROSS_WORKSTREAM_CONSISTENCY_MATRIX.csv
SHARED_IDENTITY_AND_CONTRACT_REGISTER.csv
FINDINGS_AND_REMEDIATION_REGISTER.csv
EVIDENCE_REQUEST_REGISTER.csv
CLAIM_READINESS_LEDGER.csv
AUDIT_DECISION_LOG.md
```

For each reviewed work package, issue both:

- a human-readable audit report; and
- a machine-readable audit summary.

## Claim and publication readiness

No technical milestone may enter GitHub, LinkedIn, résumé, executive brief, or marketing copy until the audit records:

- owning workstream;
- governing artifact and physical identity;
- audit disposition;
- exact permitted wording;
- prohibited wording;
- public-safe link or asset;
- date and reviewer basis.

Planned, designed, built, executed, validated, accepted, and deployed are not interchangeable terms.

## Stopping and escalation rules

Recommend an immediate cross-workstream hold when any of the following occurs:

- accepted predecessor source or contract is modified in place;
- the accepted certification database is proposed as campaign target;
- recovery is silently inserted into normal execution;
- shared keys or grains diverge without governed mapping;
- Power BI creates material durable truth with no source owner;
- M3 duplicates accepted M2 business truth;
- future data leak into as-of reporting or forecasting;
- campaign scale is changed through source edits rather than parameters;
- causal, production, regulatory, or autonomous claims exceed the evidence;
- a source chat proceeds past a mandatory gate while its audit disposition is `HOLD` or `FAIL`.

## Autonomy

Do not wait for approval on ordinary audit design, read-only validation, reconciliation, evidence organization, or report drafting. Make the audit plan, execute available controls, and issue exact dispositions. Ask Andrew only when required evidence is absent from all supplied packages or when a policy decision cannot be resolved from governing authority.

## First deliverable

Produce the complete **IA-WP0 Package Ingestion, Authority Reconciliation & Audit Baseline** and then audit the first available source-chat work package. State clearly:

- what was physically verified;
- what remains assertion-only;
- which cross-workstream invariants are frozen;
- which evidence requests are open;
- whether each workstream may continue, may continue only within a bounded design phase, or should hold.

Maintain elite professional audit documentation and the No Cold Handoffs standard throughout.

## Power BI — IA-WP2

The R2 submission closes both original shared-design findings:

- **XWS-F-001 is closed.** `module1_run_id` is the accepted physical predecessor lineage key. `run_id` and `created_by_run_id` are controlled physical aliases; `module2_run_id` is historical or proposed only.
- **PBI-F-001 is closed.** `scenario_id` is the scenario key, `scenario_code` is descriptive and nonunique, and no submitted relationship uses `scenario_code`.

Independent re-performance produced:

- 213 of 213 primary-key controls passed, with zero null or duplicate key rows.
- 318 of 318 source-parent relationship controls passed, with zero orphan distinct keys.
- 59 unique account paths reconciled to **44 applications, 44 merchants, and 15 matched stress overlays**.
- All 13 endpoint-only facts reproduced statically, with no-back-casting logic and a 2026-11-22 endpoint.
- The submitted footprint reconciled to 58 tables, 70 relationships, 124 measures, 128 queries, and 380 activation objects.

### Remaining Power BI blocker

**PBI-F-002 — `BLOCKING`**

Twenty nonempty accepted objects are outside the selected Power BI source population:

- Two have an optional-source rationale.
- Eighteen lack an object-level `include`, `defer`, `optional`, or `exclude` decision, substitute-source mapping, impact test, and activation consequence.

The audit does not conclude that all eighteen must enter the model. It concludes that their omission is not yet governed.

### Activation decision

The submitted A1 wave is too broad at 203 objects. It is divided into:

| Phase | Tables | Relationships | Queries | Measures | Boundary |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| **A1a** | 14 | 6 | 47 | 0 | Identity, lineage, calendar, control, reconciliation, and validation spine |
| **A1b** | 18 | 26 | 32 | 60 | Remaining A1 facts, relationships, and measures after A1a passes |

`AUD-SCN-004`, `AUD-REL-005`, and `AUD-ASOF-003` remain `HOLD_LOCAL_DESKTOP_EVIDENCE`.

**Exact next action:** submit the twenty-object source-selection completeness addendum and prepare A1a evidence templates. Local Power BI Desktop construction may **not** begin until PBI-F-002 closes and the independent audit issues an A1a entry authorization.

## Module 3 — IA-WP4

### Accepted design determinations

The combined original package and addendum support the following:

- M2.5–M2.10 retain authoritative operational account-day, monitoring, intervention, operational setup, servicing, lifecycle, reconciliation, certification, performance-tier, queue, and current account-state truth.
- M3 may create lineage-preserving projections and future versioned derivative portfolio, lifetime, forecast, stress, economics, allocation, and planning facts.
- M3 may not recompute or override accepted M2 operational truth.
- `module1_run_id` is the predecessor lineage key.
- `portfolio_run_id` is additive and does not replace predecessor lineage.
- The current source has one `FUNDING_MONTH_V1` vintage: 2026-07.
- Portfolio as-of and evidence cutoff are both 2026-07-23.
- Observed history is 2026-03-26 through 2026-07-23.
- The 2026-07-26 through 2026-11-22 path is deterministic synthetic mechanics, not observed performance or forecast calibration.
- Forecast-horizon identity remains deferred to M3.4.

The static DDL/DML scan identified 165 direct targets. All 165 were confined to new M3-owned objects, governed M3 control-plane objects, the new M3 schema, or temporary objects. No accepted M1, M2, reference, existing control-plane, or global G5-result write was found.

### G5 gate decision

Accepted physical G0 authority assigns:

```text
G4_BOOKING      → Module 2
G5_M3_CONTRACT  → Module 3
```

Therefore, `G5_M3_CONTRACT` is the correct Module 3 gate identity. This is a gate-name and authority decision only. It is **not** a passed G5 result.

### Proven M3.1 construction defect

**M3-F-001 — `BLOCKING`**

Program `229_msbf_m3_1_preflight.sql` intends to produce eleven required preflight rows but checks only whether any existing row has `pass_flag=false`.

It does not enforce:

```text
exactly 11 rows
exactly 11 distinct expected control codes
the complete PF001–PF011 code set
all 11 pass flags
```

Because at least one control uses `INSERT ... SELECT`, an absent authoritative source row can result in no control row being inserted. With no failing row present, the submitted final guard can still allow the preflight to commit.

That is a material fail-closed contract defect.

**M3.1 candidate construction disposition:** `FAIL`

### Database-execution readiness

The database-readiness population produced one pass and eight failures. The required construction R2 controls include:

- Signed authorization bound to exact R2 package bytes.
- Source and target database name, OID, and cluster-system identity capture.
- Distinct database names and OIDs and distinct `(system_identifier, database_oid)` tuples.
- Current and session role enforcement.
- Accepted G3 read-only role and source nonregression proof.
- Full pristine-target object inventory.
- Post-DDL schema fingerprint.
- Separate least-privilege deployment, execution, review, and finalizer roles.
- Complete write allowlist and predecessor-schema denylist.
- Exact eleven-control Program 229 cardinality enforcement.
- Immutability covering authorization, amendments, source snapshots, facts, archives, validation, stage results, and evidence, including `TRUNCATE`.
- Transaction-level evidence retention.
- Quarantine and discard/reclone after any prior committed partial state.
- Manual, contingency-only recovery.
- No `G5_M3_CONTRACT` result before M3.12.
- External reviewer evidence rather than SQL self-attestation alone.

**Exact next action:** prepare M3.1 construction R2, implement these controls, reconcile all accepted-G3-source-read-only / successor-target-only wording, and resubmit without executing any SQL.

## Cross-workstream frozen invariants

IA-WP5 freezes the following:

1. `module1_run_id BIGINT` is the accepted predecessor lineage key.
2. `scenario_id` is the relationship identity; `scenario_code` is not unique.
3. Account grain is `module1_run_id × scenario_id × merchant_application_id`.
4. Fifty-nine paths equal 44 merchants/applications plus 15 matched stress overlays.
5. Campaign identity is additive control-plane metadata and does not replace predecessor lineage.
6. Current vintage is `FUNDING_MONTH_V1 / 2026-07`.
7. Current as-of and evidence cutoff are 2026-07-23.
8. Observed history and deterministic synthetic monitoring dates remain explicitly separate.
9. The 13 Power BI endpoint facts cannot be historically back-cast.
10. M2 retains operational truth.
11. M3 may own future versioned derivative portfolio truth.
12. Power BI remains the governed consumption and presentation layer.
13. Campaign Scale owns campaign configuration, execution orchestration, and execution evidence.
14. Recovery remains manual and contingency-only.
15. `G5_M3_CONTRACT` is the M3 gate name, not an achieved result.

# M3.1 Construction R3 — Completed

The bounded **M3.1 Construction R3** package is complete and ready for resubmission to Independent Audit under **ER-M3-006** and the **ER-M3-004 continuation**.

This is a **source-owner construction and static-validation submission only**. It does not change the governing audit disposition:

| DecisionGoverning state      |                                                              |
| ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ |
| M3-WP0 design                | `PASS_WITH_OBSERVATIONS`                                     |
| `G5_M3_CONTRACT` gate name   | `PASS_WITH_OBSERVATIONS — NAME ONLY`                         |
| G5 result                    | `DOES NOT EXIST / NOT AUTHORIZED`                            |
| M3.1 Construction R2         | `FAIL / SUPERSEDED AS CURRENT CONSTRUCTION AUTHORITY`        |
| M3.1 Construction R3         | `SOURCE-OWNER STATIC SUBMISSION / INDEPENDENT AUDIT PENDING` |
| Database-execution readiness | `HOLD`                                                       |
| Programs 228–235             | `R3 SOURCES / UNEXECUTED`                                    |

The R2 audit identified five blocking defects across signed authorization, package-to-tree binding, attempt binding, temporal enforcement, privilege cleanup, quarantine identity, and end-state evidence. It authorized construction R3 only and explicitly prohibited database connection or execution.

## R3 remediation completed

### 1. Canonical signed-authorization design

R3 eliminates circular signature design:

- The authorization contains **no authorization self-hash**.
- It contains **no detached-signature-file hash**.
- The detached signature covers the complete canonical authorization bytes.
- The signature profile binds the approved public-key fingerprint and algorithm.
- Revocation status is a separate canonical document with its own detached signature and approved key.
- The exact `execution_attempt_id` is supplied by the signed authorization; the harness cannot generate or replace it.

R3 also corrected an additional circularity discovered during static construction: the authorization no longer attempts to sign the hashes of attestation files that can only be created after the authorized read-only connections occur.

Instead:

```
Signed authorization
→ binds the three attestation SQL definitions through the full execution tree

Authorized read-only connections
→ produce source, target, and quarantine attestation outputs

Offline attestation gate
→ validates exact content and computes output hashes

Attestation-validation receipt
→ binds hashes to authorization, package, tree, and attempt

Programs 228–235 and end-state evidence
→ consume that exact validation receipt
```

### 2. Signed package, complete local tree, and attempt binding

The offline authority gate now verifies, before any potential connection:

- final ZIP filename and SHA-256;
- ZIP path safety, duplicate-name absence, symlink absence, and CRC;
- embedded execution-tree manifest;
- every required execution-sensitive member;
- every member’s path, size, and SHA-256;
- absence of missing and extra execution files;
- execution-tree root SHA-256;
- Program 228–235 manifest and source-set hash;
- schema-contract and write-allowlist hashes;
- extracted local-tree byte parity;
- read-only local-tree permissions;
- exact signed `execution_attempt_id`.

The initially invoked local harness may perform only offline preparation. Any future database-capable path must re-enter through the newly extracted, signed-carrier-verified, read-only tree. This closes the R2 weakness under which a local helper and mutable local checksum could be altered together without changing the signed ZIP. The audit specifically demonstrated that R2 defect.

### 3. Temporal validity before every connection

R3 requires a fresh file-only authority gate before:

- source attestation;
- quarantine-authority attestation;
- target attestation;
- privilege-lease activation;
- every numbered program;
- every separate evidence-export connection;
- post-execution source nonregression;
- post-execution target nonregression;
- failure quarantine.

For a normal execution path, the gate requires:

```
not_before ≤ current UTC time < expires_at
revocation status = NOT_REVOKED
revocation status freshness within signed maximum age
approved revocation-key fingerprint
exact authorization, carrier, tree, and attempt identity
```

These controls execute before the first connection and before every later connection. The gate imports no database driver and contains no connection operation.

Quarantine is restricted to a separately signed failure-only authority scope that may remain available after normal execution authorization expires or is revoked, solely to contain a target already affected by a failed database attempt.

### 4. Distinct external quarantine identity

R3 requires three distinct database identities:

```
Accepted G3 source database
M3 governed successor target
External governance/quarantine database
```

The signed authorization must establish:

- three distinct database names;
- three distinct database OIDs;
- three distinct `(system_identifier, OID)` tuples;
- source and target distinctness;
- quarantine and target on the same target-administration cluster;
- a quarantine database external to the target database;
- an independently named quarantine-administration role;
- a quarantine DSN distinct from the target-administration DSN;
- target ownership and required quarantine authority;
- deployment-owner membership with `ADMIN OPTION`;
- `pg_signal_backend` authority;
- absence of preexisting bootstrap privileges on the target.

The accepted G3 database remains read-only source authority, and the M3 target remains a separately attested governed successor or clone. This preserves the frozen cross-workstream ownership, identity, date, recovery, and claim vocabulary.

### 5. Failure-safe privilege handling

R3 separates the bootstrap privilege lease from the target DDL transaction:

```
External quarantine database
→ attest pristine target-admin privilege state
→ commit attempt-bound privilege lease

Target database
→ 227C bootstrap begins transaction
→ Program 228 performs DDL
→ 228A revokes lease privileges and locks down roles
→ commit only after lockdown succeeds
```

If the target-side transaction fails, it rolls back. The external failure path then:

- revokes any surviving deployment-owner membership;
- revokes temporary target-database `CREATE`;
- revokes target `CONNECT`;
- disables target connections;
- terminates remaining target sessions;
- records quarantine terminal state;
- requires target discard and reclone;
- prohibits normal-chain resume;
- prohibits ad hoc cleanup.

R3 explicitly records the privilege lease as consumed immediately after the target-side DDL-and-lockdown transaction commits, before a separate evidence-export connection is attempted. An evidence-export failure therefore cannot falsely classify an already-consumed privilege lease as active.

Recovery remains manual, contingency-only, and separately authorized.

### 6. Exact exit and end-state evidence

The future evidence model now persists or requires:

- exact authorization ID and authorization-document hash;
- exact signed attempt ID;
- package filename and SHA-256;
- execution-tree manifest and root hashes;
- program source-set hash;
- program path and source hash;
- attestation-validation receipt hash;
- source, target, and quarantine tuples;
- intended and observed roles;
- preconnection-validation receipts and revocation state;
- program start timestamp;
- program end timestamp;
- total duration;
- process exit code;
- evidence-export exit code;
- final step exit code;
- terminal state;
- backend PID;
- transaction ID;
- transaction-identity capture timestamp;
- database result status and payload;
- database evidence-row hash;
- stdout and stderr hashes;
- last committed program;
- attempt-level final state.

A successful numbered program requires exactly one matching transaction-identity row and exactly one matching committed database-evidence row. Backend, transaction, role, target tuple, program, package, source, attempt, start time, and result status must agree.

# Finding redetermination

## `M3-F-002` — retained `OPEN / BLOCKING`

### Owner and lockdown contradiction

`227C` activates `DEPLOYMENT_OWNER_ROLE`. Program 228 then creates `msbf_m3` and its objects without an explicit `AUTHORIZATION` clause or a later owner-transfer operation.

Program `228A` subsequently requires:

1. the schema owner to equal `MSBF_M3_SCHEMA_OWNER_ROLE`; and
2. `DEPLOYMENT_OWNER_ROLE` to lack `CREATE` on the schema.

Those conditions cannot be jointly satisfied by the submitted source:

- When the two role identities differ, the exact owner check fails.
- When they are the same role, the deployment role remains the owner and cannot satisfy the intended no-owner/no-`CREATE` lockdown.

### Pre-lease grant authority

`227D` grants nine role memberships before `227F` runs its grant-authority attestation. `GA008` attests only six memberships and omits at least:

```text
MSBF_M3_SCHEMA_OWNER_ROLE
MSBF_M3_OBJECT_OWNER_ROLE
```

The deployment-owner membership is also leased without corresponding GA008 coverage.

The implementation therefore activates privileges before proving that the grantor possesses all required signed authority.

**Effect:** Program 228 cannot be accepted as statically executable under its own owner, lease, and lockdown contract.

---

## `M3-F-008` — new `OPEN / BLOCKING`

### Approved signer identity is not enforced

The normal authority gate was independently tested using:

- the exact R4 carrier;
- an audit-generated RSA key not present in any approved authority register;
- a declared public-key fingerprint that did not match the signing key;
- a signature algorithm profile that differed from the declaration; and
- fourteen unresolved authorization placeholders.

The gate returned `PASS`.

The failed-attempt gate was separately tested with another arbitrary key. It likewise returned `PASS`, despite:

- a mismatch between the declared and actual signer fingerprint; and
- a durable attempt-end-state `row_sha256` that did not match independent full-row recomputation.

The gates verify that a signature is mathematically valid against the caller-supplied public-key file. They do not establish that the key belongs to an approved authority, enforce the declared algorithm profile, or require a fully completed canonical authority document.

**Effect:** any party able to supply its own key pair can construct apparently valid execution or quarantine authority. This defeats the central signed-authorization boundary.

---

## `M3-F-003` — retained `OPEN / BLOCKING`

R4 introduces a signed failed-event model, quarantine postconditions, a full-row evidence writer, and an evidence-population sealer. The integrated path remains fail-open in several respects.

### Quarantine source and transaction boundary

The `--quarantine-only` branch does not reperform the signed-carrier and extracted-tree authority gate. It invokes the quarantine SQL from the mutable local root.

Containment actions and `QP001–QP012` are evaluated in the same transaction before `COMMIT`. Therefore:

- a postcondition failure rolls back the containment that the postcondition was intended to verify; and
- no separate post-commit connection proves that containment persisted.

No durable, independently verified quarantine receipt closes that gap.

### Evidence-row integrity

The evidence writer calculates `row_sha256` and then mutates the `database_evidence` structure. Independent recomputation over the final saved record therefore differs from the stored hash.

The harness also does not:

- invoke the supplied program-evidence export;
- integrate the observed role, backend PID, transaction ID, and database evidence into the final external row;
- parse and reconcile the captured transaction-identity CSV; or
- require a database evidence record that matches the external record.

### Evidence-population completeness

The package contains a governed evidence profile with a minimum population of 34 records. The executable sealer does not enforce that authority.

An independently constructed two-record profile containing only:

```text
OFFLINE_AUTHORITY_GATE
ATTEMPT_END_STATE
```

was accepted and sealed.

The harness builds its exact profile after execution from whichever evidence identifiers happen to exist. It does not bind the attempt to the governed class/cardinality profile, the approved evidence-seal key, the signed append-only destination, or a durable retention receipt.

**Effect:** incomplete, internally inconsistent, or incorrectly sourced evidence can be labeled sealed.

---

## `M3-F-005` — retained `OPEN / BLOCKING`

The SQL definitions now correctly require exact source `SNR001–SNR012` and target `TNR001–TNR014` control populations. The executable variable contract remains incomplete.

The authorization-to-PSQL generator does not emit:

```text
SOURCE_PRE_EXECUTION_NONREGRESSION_ROOT_SHA256
SOURCE_POST_EXECUTION_NONREGRESSION_ROOT_SHA256
EXPECTED_SCHEMA_FINGERPRINT_SHA256
```

The harness invokes the nonregression suites using only the generated base-variable population and does not add those missing values.

**Effect:** the submitted source and target nonregression paths cannot execute as designed, regardless of the correctness of their internal SQL assertions.

---

## `M3-F-007` — new `OPEN / MAJOR`

The source-owner static suite is reproducible but materially incomplete.

Its 82 static controls and 28 negative tests did not detect:

- the schema-owner and lockdown impossibility;
- missing grantor-role attestation before lease activation;
- arbitrary signer acceptance;
- algorithm and signer-fingerprint mismatches;
- unresolved completed-authority placeholders;
- missing nonregression variables;
- quarantine-tree bypass;
- precommit-only quarantine verification;
- post-hash evidence mutation;
- missing database and transaction evidence integration; or
- acceptance of a two-record evidence population.

**Effect:** source-owner PASS counts cannot independently support construction acceptance until the suite includes adversarial, cross-file executable-contract tests.

# M3.1 Construction R5 — Source-Owner Static Submission Prepared

The bounded **M3.1 Construction R5** static-remediation package has been prepared for resubmission under **`ER-M3-004`** and **`ER-M3-006`**.

R4 remains the controlling independent disposition until R5 is re-audited: M3-WP0 and the `G5_M3_CONTRACT` name-only decision remain `PASS_WITH_OBSERVATIONS`; Construction R4 remains `FAIL`; database-execution readiness remains `HOLD`; Programs 228–235 remain unexecuted; and no G5 result or public M3.1 claim is authorized.

| Decision or activityCurrent state |                                                                    |
| --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| M3-WP0 design                     | `PASS_WITH_OBSERVATIONS — UNCHANGED`                               |
| `G5_M3_CONTRACT` gate name        | `PASS_WITH_OBSERVATIONS — NAME ONLY`                               |
| G5 result                         | `DOES NOT EXIST / NOT AUTHORIZED`                                  |
| M3.1 Construction R4              | `FAIL / SUPERSEDED AS CURRENT SOURCE-OWNER CONSTRUCTION AUTHORITY` |
| M3.1 Construction R5              | `SOURCE-OWNER STATIC SUBMISSION / INDEPENDENT RE-AUDIT PENDING`    |
| Database-execution readiness      | `HOLD`                                                             |
| Programs 228–235                  | `R5 SOURCES / UNEXECUTED`                                          |
| Database connection, DDL, or DML  | `NOT PERFORMED`                                                    |
| Runtime DBR2 controls             | `0 / 20 EXECUTED`                                                  |

The R5 package preserves the accepted predecessor, grain, population, temporal, ownership, recovery, and claim boundaries: `module1_run_id`, `scenario_id`, the 59-path population comprising 44 identities and 15 overlays, the July 2026 vintage, the July 23 cutoff, M2 operational-truth ownership, manual separately authorized recovery, and a G5 gate name with no result.

## R5 source remediation

### Program 228 ownership and post-DDL lockdown

The R4 audit proved that the submitted Program 228 owner model and the `228A` lockdown assertions could not both be true. It also found that several signed grantor memberships were activated before the complete grant-authority population was attested.

R5 now implements:

- explicit `CREATE SCHEMA ... AUTHORIZATION` ownership;
- distinct signed control-schema, M3-schema, and M3-object owner roles;
- explicit `SET LOCAL ROLE` transitions for object creation;
- exact owner verification in `228A`;
- removal of deployment-role `CREATE` paths without attempting to remove the implicit rights of the actual owner;
- pairwise role-separation and no-alias controls;
- exact pre-lease sequence:

```text
227H — prove external lease-pristine state
227F — attest GA001–GA027 and every signed grantor role
227D — persist exclusive PENDING lease
227D — activate exact attempt-bound privileges
227D — transition lease to ACTIVE through compare-and-set
227C — execute the ten signed bootstrap operations
228  — create the governed schema and objects
228A — execute the nine signed lockdown operations
227G — consume the lease after exact receipt reconciliation
```

The existing signed-operation population remains exactly:

```text
227C operations       10
228A operations        9
Total                 19
```

Every operation retains its exact canonical SQL, sequence, operation hash, grantor, grantee, object, privilege, reversal operation, target tuple, authorization ID, execution-attempt ID, package identity, and execution-tree identity.

### Approved cryptographic authority identities

The R4 audit demonstrated that caller-selected keys, signer-fingerprint mismatches, algorithm mismatches, and unresolved completed-authority placeholders could pass the prior gates.

R5 introduces one immutable approved-authority registry with four separate authority purposes:

| AuthorityEnforced use |                                                    |
| --------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- |
| `AUTHORIZATION`       | Normal execution authorization                     |
| `REVOCATION`          | Authorization revocation status                    |
| `FAILED_EVENT`        | Exact failed-attempt event required for quarantine |
| `EVIDENCE_SEAL`       | Complete evidence-population seal and retention    |

Each authority is bound to:

- an exact authority code and key ID;
- an exact packaged public-key path;
- the SHA-256 of the approved DER public key;
- `RSA_PSS_SHA256`;
- the approved canonicalization profile;
- allowed document types;
- a prohibition on caller-selected substitute keys.

The normal authority, revocation, failed-event, and evidence-seal gates now reject:

- an unregistered key;
- a different key at the approved path;
- a declared fingerprint that does not match the actual DER key;
- a caller-selected algorithm;
- a canonicalization mismatch;
- an unresolved placeholder in a document claiming completed authority;
- a missing, extra, or mismatched signed-artifact hash;
- a package path or identity inconsistent with the signed authority.

The canonical authorization remains explicitly labeled `UNCOMPLETED_NOT_EXECUTION_AUTHORITY`. Its remaining placeholders therefore do not authorize anything; the completed-authority gate requires their resolution and rejects them if a document is advanced to completed status.

### Verified-tree quarantine and post-commit verification

The R4 audit found that quarantine could bypass the verified extracted tree, containment and postconditions were tested in the same transaction, and no independent durable quarantine receipt proved that containment persisted after commit.

R5 changes the future executable sequence to:

```text
Signed failed-attempt event
→ approved failed-event key verification
→ durable failed-attempt row-hash recomputation
→ final-carrier and extracted-tree verification
→ verified-tree re-entry
→ containment transaction
→ COMMIT containment
→ separate read-only post-commit connection
→ exact QP001–QP012 verification
→ durable attempt-bound quarantine receipt
→ discard-and-reclone requirement
```

The public quarantine entry cannot directly execute a mutable local SQL file. It must extract and verify the signed carrier, then invoke quarantine from the verified tree.

The independent `QP001–QP012` phase requires:

- exactly 12 result rows;
- exactly 12 distinct control codes;
- exact expected-code equality;
- no missing or additional code;
- no null result;
- `bool_and(pass_flag) = TRUE`;
- exact attempt, authorization, package, lease, and target-tuple binding.

A failure in the post-commit verification does not roll back containment. It prevents a successful quarantine receipt and leaves the target in the fail-closed discard/reclone state.

### Final-row evidence integrity

R5 integrates the governed database evidence export into every future numbered-program path.

Each program evidence record must reconcile:

- database name and OID;
- system identifier;
- observed session role;
- backend PID;
- transaction ID;
- transaction-identity timestamp;
- program code and sequence;
- exact program-source hash;
- package and execution-tree identities;
- authorization and attempt identities;
- start and end timestamps;
- process and evidence-export exit codes;
- terminal result;
- database result payload;
- database evidence-row identity.

The external writer now:

1. assembles every required final field;
2. reconciles the database evidence and transaction-identity export;
3. validates the completed row;
4. removes any preexisting derived hash field;
5. computes the canonical full-row hash once;
6. writes the final record through a create-only path;
7. prohibits any post-hash mutation.

The failed-attempt gate independently recomputes the durable failed-attempt row hash before accepting the signed failure event.

### Governed evidence profiles and append-only retention

R5 no longer creates a post hoc evidence profile from whatever records happen to exist.

The governed profiles require exact evidence-class and cardinality populations:

```text
M3_1_R5_COMPLETED_ATTEMPT_V1
Exact total records: 47

M3_1_R5_FAILED_QUARANTINED_ATTEMPT_V1
Exact total records: 6
```

The materializer and sealer reject:

- a two-record or otherwise abbreviated profile;
- missing or additional evidence classes;
- duplicate evidence IDs or sequences;
- a population inconsistent with the governed profile;
- an incorrect row hash;
- an incorrect population-root hash;
- an unapproved seal key or algorithm;
- a retention destination not bound by signed authority;
- a missing durable retention receipt.

The retention design is create-only, no-overwrite, permission-sealed, and bound to a signed append-only capability. Failure handling remains armed until the final population seal and durable retention receipt have both succeeded.

### Source and target nonregression integration

The R4 SQL definitions were individually fail-closed, but the executable variable contract omitted three required values, making the integrated paths unsatisfiable.

The R5 authorization-to-PSQL generator and harness now emit and supply:

```text
SOURCE_PRE_EXECUTION_NONREGRESSION_ROOT_SHA256
SOURCE_POST_EXECUTION_NONREGRESSION_ROOT_SHA256
EXPECTED_SCHEMA_FINGERPRINT_SHA256
TARGET_NONREGRESSION_ROOT_SHA256
```

The source and target suites retain their exact contracts:

```text
Source: SNR001–SNR012
Target: TNR001–TNR014
```

Attempt completion requires separately written, independently parsed, exact-true receipts for:

- source pre-execution nonregression;
- source post-execution nonregression;
- target post-execution nonregression.

Zero rows, duplicate rows, missing codes, extra codes, null results, false results, root mismatch, receipt mismatch, or absent variables fail closed.

### Expanded adversarial static suite

The R4 audit concluded that its prior 82 controls and 28 tests were reproducible but did not challenge the material cross-file contracts that later failed.

The R5 suite was expanded and freshly executed against the final carrier. It now contains **56 offline adversarial tests**, including explicit reproductions for:

- owner/lockdown contradiction;
- role-alias bypass;
- grantor omission before lease activation;
- missing, reordered, or altered signed operations;
- overlapping attempt-bound leases;
- arbitrary authorization and failed-event signers;
- signer-fingerprint and algorithm mismatch;
- unresolved completed authority;
- missing and inconsistent signed package hashes;
- quarantine verified-tree bypass;
- invalid durable failed-attempt hash;
- precommit-only quarantine verification;
- incomplete `QP001–QP012`;
- post-hash evidence mutation;
- missing database, role, backend, or transaction evidence;
- two-record self-defined evidence seal;
- missing approved seal key or retention binding;
- nonregression-variable omission;
- false or zero-row nonregression;
- premature failure-trap removal;
- explicit Bash invocation after ordinary ZIP extraction.

**Decision ID:** `IA-D-R5-CAM-001`

The source-owner submission correctly framed HF07 as evidence for independent redetermination—not as self-closure of `CAM-F-006`, `CAM-F-007`, or `CAM-F-009`, independent CR-WP5 acceptance, or authorization for PostgreSQL, checkpoint/report execution, recovery, or campaign replay. [original conversation file citation]

## Blocking findings

### `CAM-F-006` — trusted authority and governed adapter selection

HF07 materially improves the pre-import trust architecture. The trust gate now verifies external bootstrap inputs, signer metadata, carrier and extracted-tree parity, manifests, protected-tree identities, and the hashes of the principal runtime artifacts.

Two blocking issues remain.

First, the trust gate verifies the **hash of the 230-row adapter-binding catalog**, but it does not parse that catalog and enforce exactly one canonical row connecting:

```text
adapter_id and adapter_type
→ adapter definition
→ query or source
→ profile and profile lock
→ source plan
→ orchestrator, driver, and module
```

The individually authorized artifacts can therefore all be hash-correct while not forming one governed adapter configuration.

Second, the separately packaged bootstrap remains expressly a candidate. No signer, key, or bootstrap authority is independently approved through this review. The source-owner submission itself recognizes that the candidate does not establish signer or key trust. [original conversation file citation]

```text
CAM-F-006
FAIL / OPEN / BLOCKING
```

### `CAM-F-007` — actual same-session target binding

HF07’s generated SQL places the target checks and adapter query in one intended `psql` process and read-only transaction. However, its query validator screens SQL mutation keywords without screening the **psql command language**.

Independent faithful tests show that the final scanner accepts payloads containing:

```text
\connect unauthorized_database
\i [REDACTED_LOCAL_PATH]
\! echo audit_escape
\set x 1
```

when an allowed `SELECT` statement is also present.

A `\connect` command can replace the connection after the initial target and pristine-state checks. The production driver then verifies only that six textual markers appear once and in order. It does not:

- parse and reconcile the emitted database, OID, system identifier, role, host, or port;
- require the same backend PID at session start and session end; or
- invoke the supplied transcript-validation function against the actual production transcript.

The native connection-substitution tests exercise a detached synthetic helper, not the production driver’s captured output.

```text
CAM-F-007
FAIL / OPEN / BLOCKING
```

### `CAM-F-010` — production path is not invokable

This is a new blocking finding.

The exact production orchestrator invokes the single-session driver using:

```powershell
-CarrierPath $CarrierPath
```

The driver does not declare a `CarrierPath` parameter. PowerShell parameter binding therefore fails before the driver can reach `psql`.

The final production driver also retains:

```powershell
Import-Module -LiteralPath ...
```

HF03’s preserved correction history identifies that form as invalid in the tested PowerShell environment and states that it was replaced with `Import-Module -Name`. The native harness uses the corrected `-Name` form, but the final production driver does not. [original conversation file citation]

The 6/6 AST suite parses source syntax, imports the module separately, and exercises synthetic helpers. It never invokes the exact:

```text
trust receipt
→ production orchestrator
→ production driver
→ fake psql
```

path. It therefore cannot detect the undeclared parameter or the production import regression.

```text
CAM-F-010
FAIL / OPEN / BLOCKING
```

PowerShell was not installed in the Independent Audit runtime. I therefore did not claim an independent rerun of the submitted 6/6 and 35/35 native suite.

Instead, I independently verified:

- the exact native-evidence carrier and source identities;
- every submitted native-result row and its bound hashes;
- the source-owner static validator;
- the manifest and tree populations;
- the 230 adapter-binding identities;
- the production invocation contracts; and
- the cross-file defects above directly from the final source.

This limitation accounts for one `HOLD` control. It is **not** the basis for the `FAIL`; the blocking disposition rests on exact source-level contradictions.

## R7 remediation implemented

### External bootstrap before package-controlled code

R7 supplies a **separately delivered, package-independent bootstrap/extractor** that:

* verifies its own exact approved SHA-256;
* verifies the final carrier filename and SHA-256;
* rejects unsafe, duplicate, case-fold-colliding, symlink, encrypted, or CRC-failing members;
* verifies the complete payload manifest and `SHA256SUMS`;
* verifies the complete execution tree and ordered root;
* verifies the eight-program source manifest and ordered root;
* extracts only after all carrier controls pass;
* creates an externally signed parent receipt and fixed external single-use ticket registry.

External bootstrap/extractor [historical link target not available in this public edition]

Bootstrap SHA-256 sidecar [historical link target not available in this public edition]

```text
Bootstrap SHA-256:
82702dab156e1538bea5d801ebcd4216aa353047948c3432c57da274eb14f57c
```

Independent approval cannot be self-issued by the Module 3 source owner. R7 therefore supplies an **exact approval request** binding the bootstrap and final carrier, but does not falsely represent that Independent Audit has already approved it:

External-bootstrap approval request [historical link target not available in this public edition]

Approval-request summary [historical link target not available in this public edition]

Executable approval schema [historical link target not available in this public edition]

A source-owner test-only signed approval fixture was used to exercise the file-only mechanics. It is not execution authority or independent acceptance.

### Nonforgeable, globally single-use internal entry

Every database-capable internal entry must now present:

* the exact externally signed parent receipt;
* an externally signed entry ticket;
* the exact carrier and execution-tree identities;
* exact authorization and attempt IDs;
* exact executable path and executable hash;
* exact permitted mode;
* the fixed registry root recorded by the external bootstrap.

Ticket consumption uses an atomic move in the fixed external registry. The caller cannot choose a substitute marker path, and a second use of the same ticket fails.

Internal-entry gate [historical link target not available in this public edition]

### One executable authorization and revocation interface

R7 publishes one completed-authorization schema and one revocation schema. Both use strict field populations and reject additional or incompatible fields.

Executable authorization schema [historical link target not available in this public edition]

Executable revocation schema [historical link target not available in this public edition]

The authorization must contain an exact ordered `signed_artifacts` population equal to the complete 78-row requirement catalog. Omitted, additional, reordered, size-mismatched, or hash-mismatched artifacts fail closed.

Complete signed-artifact requirement catalog [historical link target not available in this public edition]

### One operation-root algorithm and separated chronology

R7 uses one operation-root algorithm:

```text
SHA256_ORDERED_CANONICAL_OPERATION_ROWS_V1
```

The exact population remains:

```text
227C operations:    10
228A operations:     9
Total:              19
```

The operation contract now separates:

```text
Pre-execution signed operation authority
→ privilege lease and operation materialization
→ future physical execution
→ post-execution physical result receipts
```

The pre-execution receipt contains no `pass_flag` or physical observation. The post-execution receipt cannot exist until all 19 physical operations have later produced exact observed results.

Signed operation catalog [historical link target not available in this public edition]

Canonical operation-root authority [historical link target not available in this public edition]

Pre-execution authority validator [historical link target not available in this public edition]

Post-execution physical-receipt validator [historical link target not available in this public edition]

### Satisfiable and exclusive role chain

The future authority chain is now explicitly:

```text
External lease-controller LOGIN role
→ grants attempt-bound membership in the NOLOGIN executor
  to the operation-materializer LOGIN role

External lease-controller LOGIN role
→ grants every signed per-operation grantor
  to the NOLOGIN executor for the same exclusive lease

Operation-materializer LOGIN role
→ NOLOGIN executor
→ exact signed per-operation grantor
→ physical operation
```

The operation materializer has no independent grantor membership or ADMIN OPTION. The executor is `NOLOGIN`. The lease controller’s required ADMIN OPTION population is attested before activation. Each operation must reassert the exact active lease, attempt, package, tree, target tuple, operation root, executor, and grantor.

Lease, role, and operation-authority matrix [historical link target not available in this public edition]

### Exact 47-row evidence identity

The completed-attempt profile requires exactly **47 evidence rows**. Every row must carry one identical tuple:

```text
authorization_id
execution_attempt_id
package_sha256
execution_tree_root_sha256
```

A single different attempt, authorization, package, or tree identity causes population materialization to fail.

The canonical program evidence path also requires:

* one program-execution preconnection receipt;
* one evidence-export preconnection receipt;
* one canonical `database_evidence_row_sha256`;
* exact database name, OID, and system identifier;
* observed role;
* backend PID;
* transaction ID;
* program source identity;
* process and export results;
* start and end timestamps;
* result payload.

Evidence-profile materializer [historical link target not available in this public edition]

### Exact seal-to-retention binding

R7 creates a deterministic sealed-byte bundle containing:

* the exact population manifest;
* the exact ordered 47-record population;
* the exact record bytes;
* every full-row hash;
* the population-root hash.

The signed seal payload binds:

```text
authorization
attempt
package
execution tree
manifest hash
population root
sealed-bundle hash
sealed-bundle size
retention destination
exact 47-record cardinality
approved seal key and algorithm
```

Retention revalidates those values immediately before copying and copies **only the already sealed bundle bytes**. It does not rediscover, regenerate, reserialize, or select the evidence population again.

Append-only exact-byte retention control [historical link target not available in this public edition]

# IA-WP4F — M3.1 Construction R7 independent audit completed

**Decision ID:** `IA-D-R5-M3-002`

R7’s frozen physical identity is internally coherent and matches the submitted sidecar: 215,170 bytes, SHA-256 `0bde7cfabfe16035ed40f3d55bda0e81dd474cdbb5c519a31611311d35dc2ac3`, 108 ZIP entries, 100 manifest rows, 84 execution-tree rows, eight program-source rows, and 78 signed-artifact requirements. [original conversation file citation]

The source owner correctly characterized the clean-extraction run as file-only and kept independent bootstrap approval pending; its test fixture is not execution authority. [original conversation file citation] The submission also expressly reports that no database connection, target creation, privilege activation, Programs 228–235 execution, DDL/DML, quarantine, recovery, runtime acceptance, or G5 result occurred. [original conversation file citation]

## Governing dispositions

| Decision | Independent disposition |
|---|---|
| M3-WP0 design | `PASS_WITH_OBSERVATIONS — UNCHANGED` |
| `G5_M3_CONTRACT` gate name | `PASS_WITH_OBSERVATIONS — NAME ONLY` |
| G5 result | `DOES NOT EXIST / NOT AUTHORIZED` |
| Construction R6 source | `SUPERSEDED` — historical `FAIL` retained |
| **M3.1 Construction R7** | **`FAIL`** |
| External bootstrap approval | **`HOLD — APPROVAL WITHHELD`** |
| Database-execution readiness | **`HOLD`** |
| Programs 228–235 | `UNEXECUTED` |
| `ER-M3-004` | `OPEN_PARTIAL_RESUBMISSION_REQUIRED` |
| `ER-M3-006` | `OPEN_PARTIAL_RESUBMISSION_REQUIRED` |
| `ER-M3-007` | `OPEN_PARTIAL_RESUBMISSION_REQUIRED` |
| `ER-M3-008` | `OPEN` |
| Database connection or execution | `NOT AUTHORIZED` |
| Public M3.1 or G5 claims | `NOT AUTHORIZED` |

`FAIL`, rather than `HOLD`, is required because the audit proved material defects in the submitted construction itself. The decision is not based merely on missing runtime evidence.

## What R7 successfully corrected

R7 closes the R6 physical-carrier reconciliation defect. One ZIP, sidecar, manifest, SHA256SUMS population, execution tree, program-source set, and signed-artifact catalog now reconcile exactly.

It also establishes:

- one submitted operation-root algorithm and one 19-operation population;
- separate pre-execution and post-execution receipt types;
- a shared authority, attempt, package, and tree tuple in the source-owner 47-record positive flow;
- continued M3-only write targeting for Programs 228–235;
- no accepted M1/M2/reference-schema write;
- no global `G5_M3_CONTRACT` result write; and
- preservation of the corrected canonical program-evidence hash interface.

Accordingly:

```text
M3-F-011  PASS / CLOSED
```

## Why R7 still fails

### `M3-F-012` — `OPEN / BLOCKING`

**The governed execution chain is not implemented.**

The submitted controlled harness is only sixteen lines. After checking the external-entry flag, it calls the internal-entry gate, prints a static-only success message, and exits.

It does not orchestrate:

```text
authorization and revocation
→ signed-artifact validation
→ source/target/quarantine attestation
→ role and ADMIN OPTION attestation
→ exclusive lease activation
→ operation materialization
→ Programs 228–235
→ post-execution receipts
→ source/target nonregression
→ failure quarantine
→ evidence materialization
→ evidence sealing
→ exact-byte retention
```

The five control SQL files `227F`, `227D`, `227C`, `228A`, and `227G` contain comments and a `NOT_EXECUTED_STATIC_DEFINITION` result. They do not implement the role attestation, lease mutation, operation execution, lockdown, postcondition, or lease-consumption controls they describe.

No quarantine or failed-attempt implementation exists in the submitted execution tree.

### `M3-F-002` — `OPEN / BLOCKING`

**The materializer, NOLOGIN executor, grantor, and exclusive-lease chain is still unsatisfied.**

R7 documents the intended chain, but the generated SQL directly performs `SET LOCAL ROLE` to the per-operation grantor. It does not first establish the NOLOGIN executor context.

The purported active-lease reassertion is emitted only as a SQL comment:

```text
-- R7-LEASE-REASSERT ...
```

It is not an executable check.

The pre-execution validator also accepted an unsigned, schema-unvalidated authorization in which:

```text
lease controller
operation materializer
NOLOGIN executor
every per-operation grantor
```

were all assigned the same role value.

The operation-root conflict is corrected, but the authority actually used by the nineteen operations is not proven exclusive, attempt-bound, role-separated, or actively leased.

### `M3-F-003` — `OPEN / BLOCKING`

**The external bootstrap, internal entry, evidence population, and retention chain remain bypassable.**

Independent file-only controls demonstrated that the external bootstrap:

- accepted an approval outside its signed time window;
- permitted one approval to issue multiple parent receipts and ticket populations;
- accepted case-fold-colliding and extra files in the excluded manifest directory;
- did not establish that the supplied parent private key corresponded to the approved public key;
- did not make parent issuance itself single-use;
- did not permission-seal or independently attest the extracted tree; and
- relied on a registry whose ownership and write controls are not independently established.

The internal-entry gate then accepted entry after execution-sensitive files—including the gate itself—had been changed after extraction. Its ticket binds the harness hash, not the complete current tree or gate implementation.

Recreating the mutable `unused` marker also allowed the same signed ticket to be consumed a second time.

The 47-record evidence materializer accepted semantically empty records when they had:

- the required class counts;
- contiguous sequence numbers;
- unique IDs; and
- a shared authority/attempt/package/tree tuple.

It did not require the nineteen operation records to represent nineteen exact operations, the sixteen program records to represent eight exact execution/export pairs, or the nonregression records to contain exact validated results.

Finally, the retention utility accepted a forged, self-hashed seal receipt and copied the bundle without independently verifying the approved evidence-seal signature.

**External bootstrap approval is therefore withheld.**

### `M3-F-010` — `OPEN / BLOCKING`

**Authority and physical-receipt interfaces remain self-derived rather than independently rooted.**

The pre-execution operation validator does not require:

- the executable authorization schema;
- an authorization signature;
- an approved authorization key;
- the offline-authority receipt; or
- role-separation controls.

The post-execution physical-receipt validator accepted a fabricated CSV containing matching operation IDs, hashes, grantor labels, and `pass_flag=true`. It did not require authenticated evidence of:

- target database identity;
- system identifier and OID;
- observed session role;
- backend PID;
- transaction ID;
- execution attempt;
- operation timestamp; or
- database-produced result provenance.

The offline authority gate likewise accepted a forged self-hashed artifact receipt while an execution-sensitive file in the supplied `package_root` had been changed. The gate receives `package_root`, but it does not independently revalidate those bytes.

### `M3-F-007` — `OPEN / MAJOR`

**The source-owner assurance suite remains materially incomplete.**

The packaged static validator freshly reproduced:

```text
20 / 20 PASS
```

The exact source-owner 22-test clean-extraction suite could not be rerun independently because the private test keys and signed approval fixture used for that run were not included. The submitted report remains source-owner evidence, while this audit substituted its own isolated file-only controls.

The source-owner tests do not detect the blocking conditions above because they do not invoke an implemented end-to-end control path.

**Decision ID:** `IA-D-R6-CAM-001`

The review was completed as a defensive, file-only software-assurance exercise. The submitted no-database fake-`psql` path was analyzed, but **no real `psql`, PostgreSQL connection, database operation, checkpoint/report execution against a database, recovery action, or campaign replay occurred**.

# Requested finding redeterminations

## `CAM-F-006` — retained `OPEN / BLOCKING`

The exact 230-row adapter-selection population now passes. Each current adapter is tied to an exact definition and governed profile.

The remaining defect is the **downstream trust handoff**.

After the pre-import gate completes, the orchestrator and driver consume an ordinary JSON trust receipt that is:

- not cryptographically signed;
- not protected by an enforced self-hash;
- not freshness-checked downstream;
- not consumed exactly once;
- accepted through a caller-selected receipt path; and
- not independently reconciled downstream to the approved trust-anchor hash, signer identity, or authorization validity period.

In an independent file-only test, the receipt’s trust status, trust-anchor hash, signer identity, and authorization-envelope hash were altered. Every field that the downstream code actually checks still passed.

The external-authority request also does not yet contain an approved signer key ID, public key and fingerprint, custody authority, validity period, revocation mechanism, or signed independent decision.

**Disposition:** `FAIL / OPEN / BLOCKING`

Open the independent forged-trust-receipt test [historical link target not available in this public edition]

## `CAM-F-007` — closed

R4 now physically implements the principal same-session controls:

- complete rejection of the `psql` backslash command language;
- rejection of `psql` variable substitution;
- one `psql` process;
- one repeatable-read, read-only transaction;
- six exact session markers;
- parsing of the actual production transcript;
- one unchanged backend PID;
- exact host, port, database, OID, system identifier, role, read-only state, pristine counts, adapter identity, attempt identity, and rollback reconciliation.

Seven independent transcript cases passed:

```text

```

This closes the static construction finding. It does **not** establish real PostgreSQL runtime acceptance.

**Disposition:** `PASS_WITH_OBSERVATIONS / CLOSED`

```text
Declared report result sets                                607
Driver/module references to these contract fields            0
Current Split-MSBFPsqlMultiResult implementation              0
```

The driver also invokes `psql` with `--tuples-only`, which suppresses column headers despite the exact-header requirement.

```text
FAKE_RESULT_ROW
```

The driver nevertheless records `PASS`.

The native production-path population tested `CHECKPOINT_001` and `CHECKPOINT_005`; it tested **no report adapter** and no 607-result-set splitting path.

# IA-WP4G — M3.1 Construction R8 audit completed

**Decision ID:** `IA-D-R6-M3-001`

The review was completed strictly as defensive, file-only software assurance. The submitted no-database engine and control chain were exercised without connecting to PostgreSQL, invoking `psql`, creating a target, running Programs 228–235 against a database, performing DDL/DML, invoking database quarantine or recovery, or creating a G5 result.

## Governing dispositions

| `G5_M3_CONTRACT` gate name | `PASS_WITH_OBSERVATIONS — NAME ONLY` |
| G5 result | `DOES NOT EXIST / NOT AUTHORIZED` |

| **M3.1 Construction R8** | **`FAIL`** |

`FAIL`, rather than `HOLD`, is required for construction because the audit proved material contradictions in the submitted authority, SQL, evidence, quarantine, fake-engine, and retention sources. Database readiness remains separately on `HOLD` because no database execution was requested or audited.

Those results are valid for the controls implemented. They establish carrier integrity, test reproducibility, and file-only orchestration mechanics. They do not establish correctness of cross-file contracts that the packaged tests do not challenge.

## Why R8 still fails

### `M3-F-010` — authority and receipt interfaces

**Disposition:** `FAIL / OPEN / BLOCKING`

The authority gate uses registry-selected public keys and verifies document signatures, but it accepted signed documents whose:

- declared signer key ID and fingerprint contradicted the approved registry;
- carrier filename, manifest identity, program root, operation-catalog identity, and other package metadata contradicted the physical package;
- source, target, and quarantine authorities all identified the same database tuple; and
- evidence-seal key ID was not the approved seal authority.

The external bootstrap also exposes generic receipt and failed-event signing commands. Independent file-only tests showed that these commands would sign fabricated, self-consistent inputs without first establishing a registry-issued parent, validated execution authorization, approved target authority, or receipt-type-specific semantic contract.

The exact bootstrap source identity is:

```text
db25c9a41531773aa4abb9c87602190979144ff6ecc29dde31c89994a5af6a94
```

Its narrow carrier-validation and extraction mechanics pass with observations. Its registry/signing construction fails, so runtime approval is withheld.

### `M3-F-002` — exclusive lease and operation authority

**Disposition:** `FAIL / OPEN / BLOCKING`

The nineteen-row operation catalog is physically coherent, but the database-side authority sequence is not executable as submitted:

- No submitted SQL inserts or otherwise materializes the nineteen rows into `msbf_m3_ext.operation_authority`.
- Program 227F requires exactly nineteen rows before lease activation.
- Program 227C then attempts to consume those rows.
- Program 227D grants nine role memberships, while Program 227F proves `ADMIN OPTION` for only one of those nine memberships.
- Programs 227D and 227G place `IF NOT FOUND` directly in plain SQL rather than within a PL/pgSQL block.

Programs 227C, 227F, and 228A also place `psql` variable references inside dollar-quoted `DO` bodies. PostgreSQL defines a `DO` body as a string literal, and `psql` does not perform variable interpolation within quoted SQL literals. The submitted source therefore does not have the parameter-substitution behavior its control chain assumes. [original conversation reference; no live target inferred]

### `M3-F-012` — exact production-source execution

**Disposition:** `FAIL / OPEN / BLOCKING`

R8 now has a file-only orchestrator and a success/failure fake-engine path. That is a substantial improvement over R7.

However, the fake engine does not read, parse, or execute:

- Programs 227F, 227D, 227C, 228A, and 227G;
- Programs 228–235; or
- the quarantine SQL definitions.

Instead, it accepts caller-supplied source hashes and emits modeled PASS events. Consequently, the successful fake-engine run does not detect the SQL defects above and does not demonstrate semantic equivalence to the future PostgreSQL path.

### `M3-F-003` — evidence, quarantine, and retention

**Disposition:** `FAIL / OPEN / BLOCKING`

The 47-row validator correctly enforces:

- exact row count;
- contiguous sequence;
- unique record IDs;
- shared top-level authority, attempt, package, and tree identities;
- exact record-class counts;
- nineteen operation IDs; and
- eight program execution/export pairs.

It nevertheless accepted independently modified populations containing:

- empty external-parent, authorization, and revocation payloads;
- incorrect operation roles, target identity, backend PID, and transaction provenance;
- invalid program database-evidence hashes;
- incorrect program database provenance;
- zero or false nonregression control populations;
- false schema parity;
- a contradictory terminal state; and
- a false evidence-completion payload.

The exact retained bundle contains the 47 canonical evidence-row files but omits the supporting cryptographic closure population needed for later independent re-performance, including signed authority and revocation documents, parent and entry receipts, artifact and preconnection receipts, operation receipts and signatures, seal payload and signature, and the retention receipt.

The quarantine definition also hard-codes QP007–QP012 as true; the fake engine hard-codes QP008–QP012. Its exact-code generator uses:

```sql
format('QP%03s', g)
```

PostgreSQL’s `format` width pads text with spaces, not zeroes. The exact `QP001`–`QP012` vocabulary requires an explicit zero-padding construction such as `lpad`. [original conversation reference; no live target inferred]

# External-Authority Activation and Failure-Terminal Addendum — Issuance Package Ready

The governing R7 audit accepted **CR-WP5 R5 Correction R2 construction as `PASS_WITH_OBSERVATIONS`**, but retained Campaign runtime readiness on `HOLD`. The remaining governance items are narrowly separated: external bootstrap/signer/trust approval is still unissued, and `CAM-F-013` requires terminal failure-state closure before runtime.

I did **not** fabricate an “external” signature inside the Campaign source-owner workspace. That would be source-owner self-approval—the exact condition R7 prohibited. The audit expressly states that no independently controlled root key, signer key, fingerprints, custody owner, validity interval, revocation mechanism, signed decision, or approved protected pin had been supplied, and that Independent Audit would not invent those authorities.

Instead, I prepared the complete, bounded package required for an independently controlled authority to issue that decision, together with an exact failure-terminal source delta ready to apply after the signed decision returns.

## Package result

The R7 audit package itself passed ZIP, manifest, path-safety, JSON, and CSV validation before being used as the governing basis.

## What the package contains

### Externally controlled activation issuance

The package includes working issuance and verification tooling for an independent authority to return:

* the exact authority-root public key and file-byte SHA-256 fingerprint;
* the exact authorization-signer public key and fingerprint;
* root and signer key IDs;
* custody owner and custody system;
* key-exportability attestation;
* rotation and revocation references;
* signed, time-bounded activation decision;
* independently signed revocation-status record;
* independently signed custody attestation;
* exact approved protected-pin bytes;
* exact pin SHA-256 bound into the signed decision;
* RSA PKCS#1 SHA-256 signatures over exact UTF-8/no-BOM payload bytes; and
* a complete public-only manifest and sidecar.

The issuer was tested with temporary self-test keys, and the independently implemented verifier successfully reproduced the signatures, public-key fingerprints, bootstrap identities, protected-pin hash, validity window, active revocation status, manifest, and private-key-exclusion controls. Those test keys were deleted and are not in the package.

### Exact failure-terminal source delta

R7 found that the parent attempt contained final adapter, driver, and orchestrator `FAIL` states, but the quarantine bundle was sealed earlier—while its copied adapter state remained `STARTED` and before driver/orchestrator terminal records existed.

The prepared delta uses the audit-permitted **self-contained sealed-population option**:

```text
ADAPTER_FAILURE.json
→ final adapter_attempt_state.json = FAIL
→ final driver_end_state.json = FAIL
→ driver throws
→ final orchestrator_end_state.json = FAIL
→ quarantine copy
→ QUARANTINE_MANIFEST.csv
→ QUARANTINE_SEAL.json
→ QUARANTINE_REVALIDATION.json
```

The proposed seal binds:

```text
ADAPTER_FAILURE.json SHA-256
final adapter state SHA-256
final driver state SHA-256
final orchestrator state SHA-256
quarantine manifest SHA-256
quarantine tree SHA-256
execution attempt ID
automatic_recovery_allowed = false
```

The strengthened `R5-NAT-040` control requires all three copied terminal states to be `FAIL`, carry the same attempt ID, appear exactly once in the quarantine manifest, match the parent final-state hashes recorded by the seal, and pass post-seal physical revalidation.

The package contains exact unified diffs and complete proposed replacements for:

```text
Invoke-MSBFSingleSessionReadOnlyAdapter.ps1
Invoke-MSBFTrustedAdapterAttempt.ps1
Invoke-CRWP5R5NativeOfflineTests.ps1
```

Each delta is pinned to the exact R5 Correction R2 preimage SHA-256 so it cannot silently apply to another source revision.

## Why no source/native rebuild was issued yet

The current protected pin still contains unresolved `<IA_TO_APPROVE>` placeholders. Any legitimate external approval will therefore change the protected-pin bytes and SHA-256. R7 requires rebuilding the protected pin and affected source/native identities **only after** the external decision exists.

The package therefore preserves the correct order:

```text
Independent authority issues signed activation
→ source workstream verifies signatures, validity, revocation and custody
→ approved pin is embedded
→ failure-terminal delta is applied
→ affected identities are regenerated
→ one final source carrier is built
→ exact success and injected-failure no-database paths are rerun
→ ER-CAM-006 / ER-CAM-009 addendum is submitted
```

This follows the audit’s exact permitted next action.

# External Activation Applied; Detached-Signing Handoff Ready

The independently issued activation return has been verified and applied to the R5 source. Because the approved protected-pin identity changed, the source carrier was rebuilt as required. The exact three-file failure-terminal delta was also applied, and all affected source, catalog, manifest, protected-tree, and carrier identities were regenerated.

The R7 audit had already accepted R5 static/no-database construction with observations while retaining runtime readiness on `HOLD`; it identified `CAM-F-013` as the remaining failure-terminal issue required before runtime.  The defect was specifically that the sealed quarantine copy preceded the final adapter, driver, and orchestrator `FAIL` states.
